The fact is that there are safe establishments that experience a discharge on a cost basis. Other companies, such as gastronomy, have so far paid nothing and are now taxed. From the point of view of entrepreneurship – the benefits for workers have already been presented: not 15 percent of workers receive a job per year, but 100 percent of the new provision applies – it is clear that the companies have fulfilled their obligations with the contribution. The entrepreneur is therefore not liable for the claims, but is liable to the employee pension fund. This is an extremely great advantage, which also has a favourable effect on workers, because the system is getting safer. The second – and this has already been said by Mr Finanzminister – is: Especially the sometimes cumulative payment of payments has brought many businesses into payment difficulties, into liquidity problems. The new system is continuous: It includes a contribution from month to month and will no longer bring these problems. It will therefore be easier to transfer holdings now, but it will also be easier to carry out such restructurings. And thirdly, we will have a level playing field in the economy. Why? – There have so far been companies that have not calculated the completion. If someone doesn't calculate the handling as a cost factor, what happens? – Then it may happen if the competitor has the costs inside that the other company eventually becomes insolvent. And this has also happened in practice: One third of all payments to the insolvency fund were settlement payments. That, ladies and gentlemen, belongs to the past. The new regulation is a step towards greater equality of competition. The international competitiveness of local enterprises has also been addressed. If you look at international balance sheets and compare key figures, you won't find the provision factor. So far, large enterprises have had competitive disadvantages. In the future, these demands will no longer be found in the balance sheet. Therefore, the new system also means more location advantages, more competitive advantages for the Austrian site. ParlaMint-AT_2002-06-12-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00106_d7e8940	Mr President. High house! ==References====External links== In June 1992, the National Council asked the Federal Government with a resolution to declare to the United Nations the dispute with Italy which had been pending since 1960 on the interpretation of the Paris Agreement on South Tyrol. On the basis of a report from the Ministry of Foreign Affairs, Parliament, after a thorough debate, came to believe that the package is so fulfilled that South Tyrol – I quote literally – "is guaranteed in its ethnic, cultural, social and economic resources today". This meant that the essential conditions of the Paris Treaty were to be seen as fulfilled. ==References== On June 10, 1992, in an official note to the Italian side, Austria declared the dispute over South Tyrol settled. This declaration was notified to the Secretary-General of the United Nations on the same day. High house! I think it is important to remind the public of these processes, as well as the fact that Parliament was intensely dealing with the issue of South Tyrol at the time. This is particularly true of the state-political importance which has always been attributed to the South Tyrol question with good reason. I therefore also consider it important that the Subcommittee on the discussion of South Tyrol policy continues to exist ten years after dispute resolution, which makes sense to say that the protection function of Austria for South Tyrol is still given on the basis of the Paris Agreement and is also based on a cross-party consensus. [[Complaints with the EIA, the liberals and MEPs of the Greens]] Alois Mock, who, as we all know, had a birthday two days ago and whom I would like to congratulate once again, said: "For South Tyrol's concerns, Austria's protective power will always stand up – with the power of mind and heart!" Ladies and gentlemen! Today I can only confirm this in my capacity as Foreign Minister, but also as an Austrian who has certain family ties to South Tyrol. Of course, I stand by it and, as I say, I have already shown it in the past. [[It is a surprise to the ÖVP, the Freeman and the Deputies of the SPÖ.]] At this point I can once again extend a warm greeting to a number of friends from Tyrol and South Tyrol who, led by the head of the state Silvius Magnago, are with us today. Thank you very much for attending this debate. [[General condolences.]] My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! The dynamic development of the autonomy of South Tyrol over the last ten years confirms that the resolution of the dispute was at the right time and was the right decision. Since then, as I believe, the country has been able to expand its competences considerably and even strengthen its autonomy. Things also went well for South Tyrol. ==References====External links== In January 1995, the Tyrolean parts of the country are interconnected in a single market and are increasingly developing, embedded in the European integration process. We therefore also have a reasonable framework for cross-border cooperation, particularly within this region of Europe. Regional cooperation on the Brenner, I can say, is indeed an excellent example of good regional cooperation. An additional dynamic has now been created by the Schengen Agreement, due to the disappearance of border controls at the Brenner in April 1998. I remember that this was also a great emotional moment, both for the Tyroleans and for the South Tyroleans. The participation of Austria and Italy also in the common currency of course enabled the economic cooperation between the Tyrolean parts of the country to receive further impetus. Ladies and gentlemen! We explained the dispute resolution ten years ago in the confident expectation that Italy would continue to protect and promote the legal acquis of South Tyrol after our step as our future partner in the Union, also in awareness of the common European values. I can say today: We didn't cheat! Furthermore, today there is social peace in the country of Etsch and Eisack. The co-existence of the different language groups develops and the economy flourishes. All of this not only goes back, but also very much to this successful functioning of autonomy. If the development of the last ten years has now been positive for South Tyrol, this is also due to the high quality of relations between Austria and Italy. Our experience with the open dialogue with Italy on all matters of common interest, i.e. also over South Tyrol, can be very positive. More recently, skeptics have argued that the social upheavals that have occurred in Europe and the world since the agreement on the South Tyrol package almost 33 years ago would make the South Tyrol autonomy obsolete and therefore appear to be in need of revision. I do not share this opinion. Although much has changed in the social fabric and in the consciousness of people, this does not mean that cultural identity with the essential criterion of language would no longer be a concern to today's people. Europe emphasizes – quite the contrary! – the discourse on minorities and the need to protect this identity. We had with us yesterday the Secretary-General of the Council of Europe. He has confirmed this to us very clearly once again. I believe that the cornerstones of the South Tyrol autonomy, which pursue this very goal, have proved to be a peace-making instrument. Let us look at today's world with its numerous violent conflicts over ethnic minorities! Some have also played in front of our doorstep. This helps us to determine the success of this South Tyrol solution and how important it is for us to continue in other disputes within the framework of the European Union, the OSCE – I remember the Austrian Presidency in 2000, where we had so many conflicts in the field of minorities – but also to warn the United Nations to settle peacefully and to support appropriate measures. I am convinced that this South Tyrol solution can take a real lead in resolving other minority conflicts. Today, no European politician with responsibility for languages and minorities can ignore the basic principles and the core elements of South Tyrol autonomy. Ladies and gentlemen! Allow me to make a point of principle. I have already said yesterday that there is a certain analogy between the situation of the year 2000 and today. At that time, on the one hand, there were minority conflicts in Yugoslavia and on the other hand, there was still the hope of coming to the European Union. Today, it is in a certain way similar: on the one hand, there are still major conflicts in the neighbourhood area, on the other hand, enlargement of the EU. That is why I can ask the question in the room: Would it not be right to come to a culture of past and dispute resolution here as well? – I think the answer can only be a decisive yes! [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] Austria strongly supports this EU enlargement, but I believe that it should also be a great historic step for us and for others, for neighbouring countries, to come to terms with history and thus enter the common Europe of tomorrow, into the Europe of undivided human rights. I believe that we ourselves have set important gestures in recent years to deal with our own past. That is why, I believe, we can rightly hope that other European countries with a similar view and sense of responsibility are also involved. Ladies and gentlemen! Let me end by quoting Alois Mock, who was here on 5 May. In the context of a debate on June 1992 on: "In a world of most bloody national conflicts, the solution found by Austria and Italy in the case of the conflict pending before the UN can be seen as a sign of hope throughout Europe, as a sign of a new culture of international co-existence." Ladies and gentlemen! This word has not lost its meaning today. – Thank you very much for your attention.

The Italianization of my homeland began. The German language was banned in the basement. The death march of the Austrian minority seemed to begin. The crime regime of Adolf Hitler, the National Socialism, has finally betrayed South Tyrol. The South Tyroleans were relocated, they became strangers in their home country. In 1945, after the war, the residents returned, no longer had citizenship, were rightless and strangers in their own country. In the negotiations in Paris, where we wanted to eliminate the illegal border, Gruber and Foreign Minister De Gasperi concluded an autonomous agreement, the famous Gruber-De Gasperi agreement. The autonomy, however, was not respected. Italy did not speak at the time. As a result, the dispute we did not resolve without risk ten years ago. The risk for Austria was that the death march of the minority would continue. The risk for Austria was that the Brenner border would continue to exist as an illegal border. The risk was that we would also lose our right to self-determination in this country. – It has been worthwhile for us to take this risk. Ladies and gentlemen! Today we can say that Italy has spoken. South Tyrol is a flowering country. The death march of the minority is stopped. With each census, the proportion of the German-speaking population increases again, and we are again approaching the value of 1918. The national unit is produced in the spirit of the European Union. If you're not a Tyrolean, you can't feel what it means when we're driving across the Brenner border today and don't have to show a passport anymore. There's going to be a warm heart. [[General condolences.]] The national unit is made in the spirit of Europe. The minority has become a bridge connecting Italy and Austria. We can say today that we are friendly countries. Nor will we forget how the Italian Government dealt with us, namely the Berlusconi government, not the previous ones, when all the others did not want to talk to us. We've become friends. The solution to the South Tyrol problem has contributed to this. I hope that, in the spirit of generosity on both sides of the burner, the Trentins are now also generous to a small minority represented here by Mr Nicolussi, the mayor – he is among us. There is still a small sprung Austrian minority in Trent, which does not have the language autonomy. I ask the Trentinians to treat her generously. Politics is drilling hard boards with patience and eye-size. We had everything: Patience, hard boards and eye-size. I hope that the Czechs, who also want to go to the European Union and who have been in the same state for centuries with us, will also raise this patience and the eye to resolve their problems in the way of negotiation before they come to the European Union, namely, that Beneš's acts of injustice are declared historic and that a spirit of reconciliation will be set before us. ParlaMint-AT_2002-06-12-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00106_d7e9390	Ladies and gentlemen! Above all, guests from South Tyrol are very honored! Ten years ago, the conclusion of the so-called South Tyrol package was not only the conclusion of a long, tough and often disturbed negotiation process, but it was also de facto the conclusion of an idea that existed in the conflict region that one could only build a good South Tyrol without the other in one's own country. It was the end of an idea that the disappearance of the other would be the most optimal of all variants. It was replaced by an image of cohabitation, an image of co-existence, also on the basis and after a long history of conflicts. From De Gasperi and Gruber to Kreisky and Saragat to many great personalities, some of whom today give us the honor of their presence, people have been committed to acting in the minority question with a wide look into the future, with a look into the future of living together, even though they were far from having a majority in their own group at that time. But they also had the necessary courage to convince this majority, who opposed a peaceful solution, and to work for this conviction. In 1992, the conclusion of the South Tyrol package was not a matter of course; this must be remembered. The international environment was not as favourable to a solution to a question like the South Tyrol question as it is often assumed today. There was the crisis due to the independence of Croatia and Slovenia, which gave new impetus to nationalism and nationalists, including in South Tyrol, also in Austria, and was increasingly moved not to reach the success that had already been achieved. There were protests, and there was a resurgence of a nationalist discourse. There was Bossi Padania at the time, and there was a marked turn in Italy, which of course also made it clear that it might not be possible in a few years to find consensus politicians on both sides, who were able to carry this project and to carry it out against the critics in their own ranks and in their own country. There was the abuse of the great keyword of the European region, which was misused for suffocation on the German oak. These were no doubt all disturbances that were overcome by the far-sighted politicians and politicians at the time. In 1992, a secure basis for a development in South Tyrol was created for the first time South Tyrol to be able to do its own things without antichambrating it in Vienna or Rome, and second, the consensus method, institutionalized respect for minority rights, would enable it to move towards co-existence. South Tyrol thus became a recognized model that can form a secure basis for further development and also for respect for, for example, newly created mixed identities in South Tyrol. This is also the requirement for Austria as a protective power to support these processes and remain in constant contact with these developments. To conclude: A model such as South Tyrol is a valuable and supportive model, because it is also a model for many conflict regions in Europe. In times of right-wing populism, I consider it extremely important that the words in "national questions" – under quotation marks – be chosen thoughtfully – and we have not only heard thoughtful words today – and not to put such success models at risk from a return to nationalist patterns of thought. A reality in South Tyrol cannot be embalmed, but it must be further developed on the basis of these treaties for a successful living together. Austria and Italy can be proud of this common model! Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2002-06-12-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00106_d7e23463	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! Mr Kummerer admitted that this Agricultural Law Change Act, which summarizes eleven legislative instruments and brings them into line with the Food Safety Agency, entails positive changes. Although he has criticized one of these eleven laws – the Plant Protection Act – I must remind Mr Kummerer that there is only such mutual recognition with Germany that there has been a red-green government in Germany for more than three years and that there is a minister there who is responsible for consumer protection, consumer protection and agriculture. [[Mr Gradwelt and Mr Schwemlein.]] That means – and I am pleased to say so – that you trust more competence in Austria than the green minister in Germany, because you fear that worse German products could come to the Austrian market. [[Abg. Dipl.-Ing. Sad: In November... approved!]] Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! This comprehensive reform package brings adaptations to EU law, which will increase market transparency in agricultural resources and will lead to a further approximation of the price level for means of production in the internal market. This legislative matter includes increased cross-border cooperation in the authorisation of farm equipment for further improvement of operational and food safety, as well as the implementation of the results of this cross-country cooperation through the authorisation of plant protection products from comparable countries with the highest standards, such as Germany, also for the Austrian market, further modernisation and improvement of control efficiency in the production of farm resources, the aim of granting farmers the highest possible safety in farm resources, and also an increase in the quality of farm resources and the protection of the environment by prohibiting certain raw materials in production. We value environmentally friendly and state-of-the-art plant protection products for Austrian agriculture, which have so far not been approved by us due to the smallness of the Austrian market. It is modern means that can also be used in organic farming. This will also ensure the sustainable supply of locally adapted domestic plant products, improved marketing of agricultural products by introducing international quality markings and a level playing field for domestic agriculture with European competitors. This represents an important step in the discharge plan in the field of operating resources and in the implementation of the internal market. – It is really a long series of positive measures, which we now adopt with this Agricultural Law Change Act for Austrian agriculture! It is really impossible to explain that, although we have an internal market in the food sector and that food produced in Germany, Holland, Denmark, France, Italy, Spain and Portugal under the conditions in force there, we can very well reach the Austrian market, but here Austrian farmers have to pay much higher prices in connection with farm resources. It is now trying to introduce the internal market in this area, and it is not at all a question of using more plant protection products, but rather of using the best possible plant protection products in comparable price constellations to the other European countries in the internal market. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] For the time being, only Germany fulfils these strict provisions that we can also apply plant protection products approved in Germany in Austria. There are now negotiations with Holland. – I would like to reply here to the annotation of the chemical industry in yesterday’s “Kronen Zeitung”: If industry is able to market plant protection products at similar prices, then no dealer will buy elsewhere if it is the same product. And the German Minister for Consumer Protection and Agriculture Künast has also explained that this is a very good development and will ensure that plant protection products which are approved in Austria under these strict criteria can also be transported to Germany without their own authorisation. That means: If our chemical industry is efficient, it can also conquer the German market. Furthermore, it is also about the Fertilizer Act, which has an increase in the production safety of fertilizers. In addition, the feed law is also concerned, with the corresponding new regulation intended to further increase product safety for farmers in this area. In addition, controls are becoming more efficient. The wine law is also amended. My colleague, Zweytick, will put in another appropriate amendment, which is wanted by the wine industry to maintain virus-resistant, old Austrian wine varieties for the future as well. In addition, the Forestry Promotional Goods Act is also amended. Overall, an important step is being taken with today's adopted Agricultural Law Change Act to ensure the sustainable protection of local agriculture. In the future, Austrian farmers will be able to use farm resources which their professional colleagues in the European Union have been able to work with for a long time. The new law can also apply operating resources authorised in a Member State of the EU without major red tape under precisely defined rules. Due to the smallness of the Austrian market, new, more efficient and environmentally friendly means have often not been applied for approval in the past. The Agricultural Law Change Act is a key milestone in ensuring the competitiveness of domestic agriculture, especially with regard to EU enlargement. It guarantees the highest standards of agricultural resources and brings with it a comprehensive debureaucratisation. I would like to thank our "Minister of Life" Willi Molterer for sending this agricultural operating means law to the National Council in this form! We will gladly decide on this improvement for the Austrian farmers.

[[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] Not only our farmers, but also many other Austrians live well, I would like to stress that. This legislation on agricultural law does not mean anything but more safety in the food sector and more efficient control. The creation of comparable standards on this large market of agricultural products in terms of farmers and their competitive markets is no longer fair for our Austrian farmers. I have to say to the opposition: You really have to take agriculture more seriously instead of blaming us that we are not taking it seriously enough! If we were to operate agricultural policy as unilaterally as you have, in my opinion, presented this rather inexperienced view, then this would lead our agriculture and our farmers to a dead end and to chaos! [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] As a longtime Member of this House, I have repeatedly experienced it in the agricultural debates: You're almost masochism! I mean, the farmers are not our enemy image, nor should they be your enemy image! [[Complaints with the EIA and representatives of the Free Trade Unions]] The products are excellent and easy to maintain a Europe-wide comparison! Part of this Agricultural Law Change Act also deals with the Wine Law. Finally, I would like to make an amendment which deals with the Austrian grape varieties. In short, the most important points: Important Austrian grape varieties can be kept in so-called in vitro containers in Korneuburg and are also implemented annually. The costs in this respect are 300 000 S to 500 000 S, which are co-financed by the wine growers. The price per set is between 20 S and 22 S. Of these, 10 sizes are used by reeds for storage. This is very important because we can keep good Austrian quality varieties virus-free and ensure the survival of these Austrian grape varieties in the future. We can also multiply these varieties and offer wine growers for further planting. – This is the essential cornerstone of this amendment. ParlaMint-AT_2002-06-12-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00106_d7e26530	Mr President. Minister! High house! I would like to comment on the agreement on cultural cooperation between Austria and the People's Republic of China, which is to be decided today. Culture and, above all, the exchange of culture has not been an important concern in recent years. Also, terms such as "multiculturality" and "globalization" are not only since the last year and the 11th. September words that are in all mouths. Multiculturality and globalisation, however, are more than keywords, they are facts, facts, opportunities, but also risks. Very often these developments are accompanied by uncertainty and fear, especially of fear of strangers, fear of others. Prejudices are instigated, prejudices and images of enemies also arise. Enemy images in different and new variations are poisons for international relations. This makes it clear how urgently we need to invest in the intensification of intercultural dialogue. One of the most effective means of dealing with the stranger, the other is to learn better, is the knowledge of another culture. This knowledge can only be acquired in partnership and joint work. This means that it must be a dynamic process, which can always develop, which is also subject to changes. Ladies and gentlemen! Therefore, cultural agreements with other countries are very welcome, as are those between the People's Republic of China and Austria, so that, as stated in the Government's proposal, existing friendships between the two countries can be developed, cooperation in the fields of culture, education and sport can be promoted, and understanding and friendship between the two peoples can be enhanced. It is not enough, however, to decide only here in Parliament. Last year, the foreign culture budget was drastically reduced, and cultural institutions were closed or cut in their expenses. This exchange must therefore also be worth the State, and money must be made available for it. Ladies and gentlemen! Very often there is a risk that foreign culture will only serve as a framework programme and will thus be given the opportunity to achieve a real impact. That is why I would like to ask you to agree to this agreement, but to continue to focus on foreign culture policy in the future, because I believe that this is a very important area in which there is still much to do, especially when it comes to foreign culture policy towards the future EU countries, the candidate countries. Thank you.

That is, when implementing this cultural agreement, I expect Austria to address differences in China itself, for example, the topic of Tibet or the autonomous region of Xinjiang. In my opinion, this too is covered by a cultural agreement, which also strengthens all those groups of persons who may not share the same opinion as the government of a country. I very much hope that in implementing this cultural agreement these areas will also take place in cultural cooperation. One opportunity to address these circumstances would, for example, be the Olympic Games, which will take place in China in 2008. In the preparations for this, very probably also in cooperation in the field of sport, in the field of youth work, it would be appropriate to do so in order to strengthen those in China who contribute to greater democratisation, which will then also promote culture again. Thank you.
